# PDF Skill

## When to use
- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Workflow
1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` if available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` (or `pypdf`) for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on it for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Temp and output conventions
- Use `tmp/pdfs/` for intermediate files; delete when done.
- Write final artifacts under `output/pdf/` when working in this repo.
- Keep filenames stable and descriptive.

## Dependencies (install if missing)
Prefer `uv` for dependency management.

Python packages:
```
uv pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```
If `uv` is unavailable:
```
python3 -m pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```
System tools (for rendering):
```
# macOS (Homebrew)
brew install poppler

# Ubuntu/Debian
sudo apt-get install -y poppler-utils
```

If installation isn't possible in this environment, tell the user which dependency is missing and how to install it locally.

## Environment
No required environment variables.

## Rendering command
```
pdftoppm -png $INPUT_PDF $OUTPUT_PREFIX
```

## Quality expectations
- Maintain polished visual design: consistent typography, spacing, margins, and section hierarchy.
- Avoid rendering issues: clipped text, overlapping elements, broken tables, black squares, or unreadable glyphs.
- Charts, tables, and images must be sharp, aligned, and clearly labeled.
- Use ASCII hyphens only. Avoid U+2011 (non-breaking hyphen) and other Unicode dashes.
- Citations and references must be human-readable; never leave tool tokens or placeholder strings.

## Final checks
- Do not deliver until the latest PNG inspection shows zero visual or formatting defects.
- Confirm headers/footers, page numbering, and section transitions look polished.
- Keep intermediate files organized or remove them after final approval.

Verdict
Split. The residual objection is not merely the unavoidable price of scope. It is a correctable packaging defect: the manuscript contains a short, field-facing Pisot-transient paper inside a much longer, independently coherent paper about one cyclic language-rank recoding.
The present title advertises the map-specific threshold and inverse-depth narrative, even though the bounded-zero theorem is now the strongest externally recognizable result.  The manuscript itself confirms the structural separation: Section 2 proves the fixed-system theorem, sharpness and arbitrary-alphabet bounded-zero theorem, while Sections 3â€“7 develop Fibonacci, metallic, quadratic and simple-Parry classifications. 
My recommendation is therefore:

Extract the Section 2 theorem package as one paper; rebuild Sections 3â€“7 as a separate cyclic-rank classification paper. Submit the extracted paper to ETDS.

I would not submit the 67-page version as assembled, and I would not disguise the split by putting thirty-plus pages of independent theorems into â€œsupplementary material.â€

Prove the arbitrary-alphabet adjacent-collapse lemma.

Introduce the cyclic rank recoding and prove the exact collision-graph bridge.


Deduce (A1).

That order is mathematically available already. Theorem 2.6 deliberately uses no canonical rank, legal language, cyclic reduction, image shift or decoder.  The manuscript also explicitly records this logical independence.

2. What the restructured article is worth
These are probabilities of clearing the journalâ€™s substantive significance-and-coherence bar, conditional on the correctness and priority assessments already reached. They are not historical acceptance-rate estimates.

The unsplit recast would therefore improve the paper materially: roughly nine points at TAMS and ten at ETDS. But it would still require severe compression of the Fibonacci and metallic prototypes, and it would still carry a second exact-classification narrative after the central theorem.
The split gives a larger improvement: approximately fifteen points over the assembled paper at TAMS and sixteen at ETDS.
Venue order
The restructuring does not reverse the venue order. ETDS remains the right first target.
For the extracted paper, TAMS becomes a legitimate ambitious submission rather than a speculative one. Nevertheless, a theorem about overlap transients, finite-state collision chains, inverse depth and Pisot symbolic numeration still has a more natural specialist readership at ETDS. At my estimates, the approximately twenty-point difference is too large for me to call TAMS the better first decision.
3. Split versus restructure
The split is better.
Value of Article A: bounded-zero transients and fixed-system sharpness


Likely length: 20â€“24 pages


TAMS substantive-bar probability: 53%


ETDS substantive-bar probability: 74%


Its claim is clean:


bounded overlap chains of U-representations of zero have an effective cycle-or-linear-transient dichotomy;


cyclic language-rank recodings inherit an aperture-linear future-only inverse bound;


one fixed cubic recurrence attains the linear order exactly.


That is one theorem package, one mechanism and one sharpness example.
Value of Article B: quadratic/simple-Parry exact classifications
After removing the general transient paper and consolidating the redundant special cases:


Likely length: 36â€“42 pages


TAMS substantive-bar probability: 27%


ETDS substantive-bar probability: 57%


This remains independently publishable. It has a coherent thesis of its own:


exact quadratic-Pisot thresholds;


chamber duality and causal-length distinctions;


exact Fischer covers and Markov order;


the coordinate-difference quotient of the classical pair graph;


exact unbounded cubic inverse depth in Bassinoâ€™s family.


The manuscriptâ€™s conclusion already presents the quadratic classification, simple-Parry graph theorem and Bassino theorem as distinct conclusions after the fixed-system theorem. 
This therefore satisfies the same criterion that made splitting correct in the sibling case: the central paper rises materially, while the removed material retains independent publication value. It is not a case where the residue would become notes, examples or an unusable appendix.
The lifecycle cost is also tolerable. Article B needs a new introduction and substantial consolidation of Sections 3â€“5, but it does not need a new theorem. The two papers would share only a short definition of cyclic language-rank recoding and a scope disclaimer.
4. Does the restructuring cost mathematics?
No. In particular, it does not weaken the fixed-system sharpness of (A2)â€“(A3).
The proof of (A2)â€“(A3) is already self-contained within Section 2. It uses:


the recurrence for Qjâ€‹(Î¸);


the direct carry bound;


the explicit contraction threshold;


the suffix-sum recurrence;


the exact terminal equations;


the m=4,5 exhaustion.


It does not invoke the Fibonacci, metallic or quadratic threshold sections. The later material supplies comparisons, not logical support.
More strongly, the proof of Theorem 2.6 explicitly says that the words Emâ€‹, read merely as coefficient words, give the sharp lower-order paths independently of their realization as differences of raw cyclic-rank inputs.  Thus the same fixed cubic object witnesses sharpness on both sides of the paper:


for causal inverse depth in (A1);


for bounded-zero transient length in Theorem 2.6.


That dual role becomes more visible, not less visible, after extraction.
What is lost is only comparative context. Article A should retain about two pages explaining:


the distinction between fixed-system sharpness and the varying-system Bassino phenomenon;


that the fixed cubic is a canonical simple-Parry example but does not need the general simple-Parry theory;


that quadratic systems admit much smaller exact thresholds, without reproducing their classification.


A citation to Article B, or to its preprint if the papers are developed concurrently, is sufficient. No metallic or quadratic proof is needed for a referee to understand what (A2)â€“(A3) establish.
5. My decision as an author
I would split, and I would submit Article A to ETDS.
I would not submit the assembled 67-page manuscript to TAMS first. The present package makes a TAMS referee decide whether one field-facing theorem justifies fifty pages of additional theorems about a newly defined recoding. That is an avoidable test of patience and framing rather than a test of the strongest mathematics.
I would also not choose the compromise of a 40â€“44-page recast unless there were a strong nonmathematical reason to keep one publication. It improves the odds but leaves value on the table.
The extracted paper gives the bounded-zero theorem the status it has earned, makes (A1) an exact application rather than the paperâ€™s definitional centre, and lets (A2)â€“(A3) close the article with fixed-system sharpness. The companion then becomes an honest exact-classification paper rather than a very large downstream section.
So the single recommendation is:

Split now; title the first paper around linear overlap transients for bounded zero representations; keep the complete fixed cubic sharpness proof in it; submit that paper to ETDS.

Verdict
Split. The residual objection is not merely the unavoidable price of scope. It is a correctable packaging defect: the manuscript contains a short, field-facing Pisot-transient paper inside a much longer, independently coherent paper about one cyclic language-rank recoding.
The present title advertises the map-specific threshold and inverse-depth narrative, even though the bounded-zero theorem is now the strongest externally recognizable result.  The manuscript itself confirms the structural separation: Section 2 proves the fixed-system theorem, sharpness and arbitrary-alphabet bounded-zero theorem, while Sections 3â€“7 develop Fibonacci, metallic, quadratic and simple-Parry classifications. 
My recommendation is therefore:

Extract the Section 2 theorem package as one paper; rebuild Sections 3â€“7 as a separate cyclic-rank classification paper. Submit the extracted paper to ETDS.

I would not submit the 67-page version as assembled, and I would not disguise the split by putting thirty-plus pages of independent theorems into â€œsupplementary material.â€
1. The concrete restructuring
I would call it a core-theorem extraction with a family-classification companion.
Article A: the extracted central paper
Proposed title

Linear overlap transients for bounded zero representations in Pisot numeration: sharp inverse depth for cyclic rank recodings

This reverses the present hierarchy. The standard-object theorem appears before the map-specific consequence.
Recommended logical order


Define one-position overlap chains of bounded U-representations of zero.


Prove the arbitrary-alphabet adjacent-collapse lemma.


Prove the linear transient theorem for those chains.


Introduce the cyclic rank recoding and prove the exact collision-graph bridge.


Deduce (A1).


Prove the fixed cubic theorem (A2)â€“(A3), which simultaneously establishes sharpness for the recoding theorem and for the bounded-zero theorem.


That order is mathematically available already. Theorem 2.6 deliberately uses no canonical rank, legal language, cyclic reduction, image shift or decoder.  The manuscript also explicitly records this logical independence. 
Current numbered results retained in Article A
Current itemDisposition in the extracted paperLemma 2.1, exact collision quotient for URetain as the bridge from the standard overlap graph to causal inverse depth.Theorem 2.3, containing (A1)â€“(A3)Retain all mathematics, but divide it editorially: (A1) becomes a corollary of the bounded-zero theorem and Lemma 2.1; (A2)â€“(A3) become a separate headline sharpness theorem.Lemma 2.5, arbitrary bounded-alphabet adjacent collapseRetain as the principal technical lemma.Theorem 2.6, linear transient boundPromote to the first main theorem and place it in the title and opening paragraph.
Theorem 2.3 currently combines the general implication, exact fixed-system depth and terminal obstruction classification in one statement.  Separating its general and sharpness parts would make the theorem hierarchy much easier for an editor to see.
Current numbered results retained as content but not as separate results


Lemma 2.2 should be absorbed as the specialization D=dUâ€‹ of Lemma 2.5. Keeping both makes the general theorem look appended rather than fundamental.


Corollary 2.4 should be folded into the discussion immediately after Theorem 2.6. Its global chain dichotomy is useful, but once Theorem 2.6 is the headline it no longer needs its own theorem number.


This leaves a compact paper with approximately five principal numbered statements rather than six nearly adjacent formulations of the same mechanism.
What belongs in Article Aâ€™s supplement
No substantive theorem from current Sections 3â€“7 should move there. Article Aâ€™s supplement should contain only audit material:


exact small-aperture search records used in the effective constants;


the seven-edge regression case for the repaired edge formulation;


expanded root-isolation arithmetic for the fixed cubic, if the authors prefer to shorten that calculation in print;


saved output from the arbitrary-D, non-Condition-F falsification batteries;


implementation and reproducibility details.


The proof of (A2)â€“(A3), including the terminal classification, must remain in the main paper. It is not optional evidence; it is the sharpness theorem.
Article B: the family-classification paper
Proposed title

Exact overlap thresholds and future-only inverse depth for cyclic ranks of quadratic and simple-Parry Î²-languages

All current results from Proposition 3.1 through Theorem 7.9 belong to this companion, not to Article A.
The second paper should not preserve every present theorem number. Its real spine should be:


Lemma 4.2, Theorem 4.3, Proposition 4.6 and Theorem 4.7 as the explicit Fibonacci model;


Lemma 6.1, Proposition 6.2, Lemmas 6.3â€“6.4 and Theorems 6.5â€“6.9 as the full quadratic classification and exact local structure;


Theorem 7.2, Corollary 7.3, Theorem 7.4, Proposition 7.5, Lemmas 7.6â€“7.8 and Theorem 7.9 as the simple-Parry quotient and cubic unboundedness package.


The following should be folded into definitions, proofs or consequence paragraphs rather than remain separately numbered:


Proposition 3.1 and Theorem 3.2;


Theorem 4.1;


Corollaries 4.4, 4.5, 4.8 and 4.9;


the Fibonacci-specialized content of Theorem 4.10, which is covered more generally later;


Lemmas 5.1 and 5.2;


Lemma 5.6, which is a standard Fischer-cover criterion;


Proposition 7.1, whose ideal-degree conclusion is immediate.


The metallic package should also be compressed. Theorems 5.3â€“5.5 should become one metallic corollary or worked subsection of Theorem 6.5, and Theorem 5.7 should be absorbed into the general Fischer-cover result, Theorem 6.9. The manuscript already identifies the negative-chamber b=1 case with the metallic construction, and the full quadratic theorem then subsumes its threshold classification.  
The existing 15-page supplement should accompany Article B. Its entropy, Blackwell, pressure, fibre, discrepancy, bounded-multiple, zeta and geometric consequences concern the cyclic recoding package, and the manuscript already says that none enters the central proof chain. 
I count 43 theorem/proposition/lemma/corollary items in the attached 67-page main PDF. I infer that the stated total of 52 includes nine in the separate 15-page supplement. Since that supplement was not attached here, I cannot responsibly assign its nine exact result numbers; their topic-level destination is nevertheless unambiguous.
2. What the restructured article is worth
These are probabilities of clearing the journalâ€™s substantive significance-and-coherence bar, conditional on the correctness and priority assessments already reached. They are not historical acceptance-rate estimates.
FormLikely main lengthTAMSETDSAs assembled67 pages, plus 15-page supplement38% â€” plausible band 33â€“43%58% â€” plausible band 53â€“63%Best possible unsplit theorem-first recast40â€“44 pages, plus 12â€“18 pages of appendices/supplement47% â€” band 42â€“52%68% â€” band 63â€“73%Recommended extracted Article A20â€“24 pages, plus 4â€“6 pages of technical audit material53% â€” band 48â€“58%74% â€” band 69â€“79%
The unsplit recast would therefore improve the paper materially: roughly nine points at TAMS and ten at ETDS. But it would still require severe compression of the Fibonacci and metallic prototypes, and it would still carry a second exact-classification narrative after the central theorem.
The split gives a larger improvement: approximately fifteen points over the assembled paper at TAMS and sixteen at ETDS.
Venue order
The restructuring does not reverse the venue order. ETDS remains the right first target.
For the extracted paper, TAMS becomes a legitimate ambitious submission rather than a speculative one. Nevertheless, a theorem about overlap transients, finite-state collision chains, inverse depth and Pisot symbolic numeration still has a more natural specialist readership at ETDS. At my estimates, the approximately twenty-point difference is too large for me to call TAMS the better first decision.
3. Split versus restructure
The split is better.
Value of Article A: bounded-zero transients and fixed-system sharpness


Likely length: 20â€“24 pages


TAMS substantive-bar probability: 53%


ETDS substantive-bar probability: 74%


Its claim is clean:


bounded overlap chains of U-representations of zero have an effective cycle-or-linear-transient dichotomy;


cyclic language-rank recodings inherit an aperture-linear future-only inverse bound;


one fixed cubic recurrence attains the linear order exactly.


That is one theorem package, one mechanism and one sharpness example.
Value of Article B: quadratic/simple-Parry exact classifications
After removing the general transient paper and consolidating the redundant special cases:


Likely length: 36â€“42 pages


TAMS substantive-bar probability: 27%


ETDS substantive-bar probability: 57%


This remains independently publishable. It has a coherent thesis of its own:


exact quadratic-Pisot thresholds;


chamber duality and causal-length distinctions;


exact Fischer covers and Markov order;


the coordinate-difference quotient of the classical pair graph;


exact unbounded cubic inverse depth in Bassinoâ€™s family.


The manuscriptâ€™s conclusion already presents the quadratic classification, simple-Parry graph theorem and Bassino theorem as distinct conclusions after the fixed-system theorem. 
This therefore satisfies the same criterion that made splitting correct in the sibling case: the central paper rises materially, while the removed material retains independent publication value. It is not a case where the residue would become notes, examples or an unusable appendix.
The lifecycle cost is also tolerable. Article B needs a new introduction and substantial consolidation of Sections 3â€“5, but it does not need a new theorem. The two papers would share only a short definition of cyclic language-rank recoding and a scope disclaimer.
4. Does the restructuring cost mathematics?
No. In particular, it does not weaken the fixed-system sharpness of (A2)â€“(A3).
The proof of (A2)â€“(A3) is already self-contained within Section 2. It uses:


the recurrence for Qjâ€‹(Î¸);


the direct carry bound;


the explicit contraction threshold;


the suffix-sum recurrence;


the exact terminal equations;


the m=4,5 exhaustion.


It does not invoke the Fibonacci, metallic or quadratic threshold sections. The later material supplies comparisons, not logical support.
More strongly, the proof of Theorem 2.6 explicitly says that the words Emâ€‹, read merely as coefficient words, give the sharp lower-order paths independently of their realization as differences of raw cyclic-rank inputs.  Thus the same fixed cubic object witnesses sharpness on both sides of the paper:


for causal inverse depth in (A1);


for bounded-zero transient length in Theorem 2.6.


That dual role becomes more visible, not less visible, after extraction.
What is lost is only comparative context. Article A should retain about two pages explaining:


the distinction between fixed-system sharpness and the varying-system Bassino phenomenon;


that the fixed cubic is a canonical simple-Parry example but does not need the general simple-Parry theory;


that quadratic systems admit much smaller exact thresholds, without reproducing their classification.


A citation to Article B, or to its preprint if the papers are developed concurrently, is sufficient. No metallic or quadratic proof is needed for a referee to understand what (A2)â€“(A3) establish.
5. My decision as an author
I would split, and I would submit Article A to ETDS.
I would not submit the assembled 67-page manuscript to TAMS first. The present package makes a TAMS referee decide whether one field-facing theorem justifies fifty pages of additional theorems about a newly defined recoding. That is an avoidable test of patience and framing rather than a test of the strongest mathematics.
I would also not choose the compromise of a 40â€“44-page recast unless there were a strong nonmathematical reason to keep one publication. It improves the odds but leaves value on the table.
The extracted paper gives the bounded-zero theorem the status it has earned, makes (A1) an exact application rather than the paperâ€™s definitional centre, and lets (A2)â€“(A3) close the article with fixed-system sharpness. The companion then becomes an honest exact-classification paper rather than a very large downstream section.
So the single recommendation is:

Split now; title the first paper around linear overlap transients for bounded zero representations; keep the complete fixed cubic sharpness proof in it; submit that paper to ETDS.

# TAMS submission metadata

Primary target: Transactions of the American Mathematical Society (TAMS)

Venue framing: ambitious first submission; acceptance is not presumed, and a
referee may regard the cyclic rank map as specialized. The bounded-zero
theorem makes TAMS somewhat more defensible because the manuscript is no
longer wholly about a bespoke cyclic rank-fold, but TAMS is still neither a
natural nor a secure placement, and this is not a tier change. The paper is
still 67 pages with 52 numbered results, while its title and most of its
architecture remain centered on cyclic language-rank recoding. A hostile
referee can therefore still ask whether the narrow field-level theorem is
large enough relative to the total package.

Strong natural alternative: Ergodic Theory and Dynamical Systems (ETDS),
which remains the venue where the full symbolic-dynamical package is most
naturally valued.

Conservative alternative: Discrete and Continuous Dynamical Systems - Series A
(DCDS-A)

Submission system: AMS Peer Review portal

Manuscript title:
Overlap thresholds and unbounded future-only inverse depth for cyclic ranks of
Pisot beta-languages

Article type:
Research article

Authors:
1. Haobo Ma
   Email: auric@aelf.io
   Affiliation: AELF PTE LTD.
   Address: #14-02, Marina Bay Financial Centre Tower 1, 8 Marina Blvd,
   Singapore 018981, Singapore

2. Wenlin Zhang
   Email: e1327962@u.nus.edu
   Affiliation: National University of Singapore
   Address: Singapore
   Corresponding author: yes

Abstract:
We study cyclic recodings of greedy Pisot numeration systems in which
ordered-language ranks are extended to the full digit cube, reduced modulo
the number of canonical words, and overlapped along consecutive windows. For
every fixed strictly increasing Pisot linear numeration system U with u_0=1,
canonical greedy digit set, and canonical normal words, we prove that there is
an effectively computable C_U such that injectivity at aperture m implies
future-only inverse length at most C_U m. No Condition F or zero-preservation
hypothesis is required. The proof uses an explicit finite carry interval,
algebraic separation, and contraction in the Pisot conjugates rather than an
exponential state count. The order is sharp in one fixed binary cubic system:
for the leading root theta of x^3-2x^2+x-1, every m at least 4 is injective and
the exact inverse length is 2 floor(m/2)-1, with penultimate obstruction set
exactly {+/- E_m} and the next set empty. Independently of the cyclic code,
we prove that for every fixed coefficient bound D, the finite overlap graph
of bounded U-representations of zero either has a reachable cycle or has
transient length less than C(U,D)m; this order is sharp for a fixed cubic
recurrence over {-1,0,1}. We also classify the overlap
threshold for every quadratic Pisot base, identify exact Fischer covers and
Markov orders, and give an exact arithmetic pair-graph quotient for
nonintegral simple-Parry bases. The results concern this cyclic language-rank
recoding, not numerical beta-normalization in general.

Novelty statement:
To the authors' knowledge, this is the first aperture-linear inverse-depth
bound for cyclic language-rank recodings over fixed Pisot numeration systems.

2020 Mathematics Subject Classification:
Primary: 37B10, 37B40; Secondary: 11B39, 37A35, 11A67, 11K38

Pisot linear numeration; beta-language; ordered-language rank; cyclic
recoding; symbolic dynamics; future-only inverse; collision graph; Fischer
cover

## Assessed Structural Alternative - Awaiting Author Decision

This is additional decision information only; it does not change the recorded
target or status above. The assessor's definite verdict was to **SPLIT**, and
he stated explicitly that the residual scope objection is a correctable
packaging defect rather than the price of scope. Extracted Article A - the
Section 2 package: the fixed-system theorem, its sharpness, and the
arbitrary-alphabet bounded-zero theorem - was estimated at 20-24 pages plus
4-6 pages of technical audit material, with TAMS 53 percent and ETDS 74
percent. The article as assembled was assessed at 38 percent at TAMS and 58
percent at ETDS. A theorem-first recast without splitting, at 40-44 pages, was
assessed at 47 and 68 percent, and the assessor judged that it leaves value on
the table.

The assessor records no mathematical cost: the proof of the fixed-system
sharpness is already self-contained in Section 2, and about two pages of
comparative context plus a citation to the companion paper suffice. His
author-level decision was: split now, title the first paper around linear
overlap transients for bounded zero representations, keep the complete fixed
cubic sharpness proof in it, and submit to ETDS. Full report:
`artifacts/oracle_sprint_A3_structure_value.md`.

\begin{document}
\begin{letter}{Editors\\Transactions of the American Mathematical Society}

\opening{Dear Editors,}

We submit our manuscript entitled

\medskip
\centerline{\textbf{Overlap thresholds and unbounded future-only inverse depth}}
\centerline{\textbf{for cyclic ranks of Pisot $\beta$-languages}}
\medskip

\noindent for consideration in \textit{Transactions of the American
Mathematical Society}.

The paper studies a cyclic ordered-language-rank recoding of greedy
numeration systems as a finite-window factor code.  The map reduces integer
language ranks modulo the number of canonical words and is not numerical
$\beta$-normalization in $\mathbb Z[\beta]$.  Its principal theorem is a
fixed-system structural estimate over a recognized Pisot numeration class:
for every strictly increasing Pisot linear numeration system $U$ with
$u_0=1$, canonical greedy digits, and canonical normal words, there is an
effectively computable $C_U<\infty$ such that
\[
 \Phi_{U,m}\text{ injective}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le C_Um.
\]
No Condition~F or zero-preservation hypothesis is required.  The proof uses
an explicit finite carry interval, separation in the dominant embedding, and
contraction in every Pisot conjugate direction, followed by finitely many
exact small-aperture graph searches.

The contraction also yields a code-independent theorem for every fixed
coefficient bound $D$: in the finite overlap graph of bounded
$U$-representations of zero, absence of a reachable cycle forces every
nonzero path to have fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.  This statement does not use
the legal representation language, cyclic rank map, or a factor code, and
its linear order is sharp for a fixed cubic recurrence over
$\{-1,0,1\}$.

The growth order is sharp within one fixed binary cubic system.  For the
leading root $\theta$ of $x^3-2x^2+x-1$, every aperture $m\ge4$ is injective,
the optimal future-only inverse length is
$2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-1$, the penultimate obstruction set is exactly
$\{\pm E_m\}$, and the next obstruction set is empty.  Thus no theorem valid
for every fixed system in the general class can replace $O_U(m)$ by $o(m)$.
This does not claim numerical optimality of the computed constant or a
universal leading coefficient.

The manuscript also gives a complete two-chamber threshold classification
for all quadratic Pisot bases, identifies the resulting overlap
presentations as right Fischer covers with exact Markov order, and develops
an exact arithmetic quotient of the classical pair graph for nonintegral
simple-Parry bases.  The latter yields a complementary cubic family with
unbounded exact future-only inverse depth.

To the authors' knowledge, this is the first aperture-linear inverse-depth
bound for cyclic language-rank recodings over fixed Pisot numeration systems.
The claim is deliberately limited to this prescribed recoding: the paper does
not claim a theorem about numerical $\beta$-normalization in general,
arbitrary $\beta$-transformations, or all Pisot symbolic dynamics.  The
manuscript gives a concentrated comparison with finite Pisot normalization,
bounded-zero automata, bounded-delay synchronization, Ashley's
state-count-linear decoder bounds, and classical pair/fiber graphs.

We believe the combination of an effective class-wide theorem, fixed-system
sharpness, and exact arithmetic classifications is appropriate for
\textit{Transactions}.  The paper's map is specialized, and the letter does
not claim otherwise; the contribution is the new quantitative mechanism and
its sharp realization across a classical numeration class.

All authors have approved this submission and declare no competing interests.

\closing{Sincerely,}

\end{letter}
\end{document}

\section{Introduction}\label{sec:intro}

Finite-window factor codes can collapse individual words and nevertheless
become invertible when successive windows overlap.  For recodings generated
by Pisot numeration, we ask how much future output is required by an inverse
with memory zero.  Our principal result is an effective aperture-linear bound
for every fixed strictly increasing Pisot linear numeration system, with no
Condition~F or zero-preservation assumption.  A single fixed cubic system
attains the linear growth order.

Fix a greedy numeration language and let $u_m$ be the number of its legal
words of length $m$.  Order those words colexicographically and identify them
with $\{0,\ldots,u_m-1\}$.  Evaluate the same positional rank on the full
digit cube, reduce it modulo $u_m$, return the legal word with that residue,
and overlap the resulting windows along the shift.  We call the resulting
sliding code a \emph{cyclic language-rank recoding}.  The positional rank is
established numeration theory
\cite{BertrandMathis1989,BruyereHansel1997,LecomteRigo2001,
CharlierCisterninoStipulanti2022}; cyclic reduction at a fixed length is the
map-specific operation studied here.

After relabeling each output word by its rank, the local rule is the modular
weighted sliding block code
\[
(x_0,\ldots,x_{m-1})\longmapsto
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_jx_j\pmod {u_m}.
\]
The relabeling is symbolwise bijective.  The rule is not a linear cellular
automaton, because its input alphabet is only the digit subset of
$\mathbb Z/u_m\mathbb Z$, not a module on which the rule is a group
endomorphism.  Likewise, $\ell_{\rm cau}-1$ is exactly the least anticipation
of an inverse sliding block code constrained to have memory zero.  This is a
standard invariant under that convention, not a right-closing delay or a
general closing constant.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{The fixed-system theorem and sharpness.}
Let $U=(u_j)$ be any strictly increasing Pisot numeration system with
$u_0=1$, canonical greedy digit set, and canonical normal words.  Theorem
\ref{thm:pisot-linear-depth-sharpness}, stated and proved immediately after
this introduction, gives an effectively computable $C_U<\infty$ such that
\[
\Phi_{U,m}\text{ injective}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le C_Um. \tag{A1}
\]
Every row carry is bounded directly by the recurrence growth constants.  A
finite algebraic separation and the contracting conjugates then collapse
adjacent carries beyond an effective aperture.  The finitely many smaller
apertures are handled by exact acyclic longest-path searches.  Thus the proof
does not use Condition~F, zero preservation, or a bounded-zero automaton, and
it avoids the generic exponential vertex count $(2d_U+1)^{m-1}$.

The same theorem fixes the binary cubic system associated with the leading
root $\theta$ of $x^3-2x^2+x-1$ and proves, for every $m\ge4$,
\[
\Phi_{\theta,m}\text{ is injective},\qquad
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-1. \tag{A2}
\]
More precisely, the penultimate obstruction set is exactly
$\{E_m,-E_m\}$ and the next obstruction set is empty.  This is the signed
terminal classification in \textup{(A3)}.  Consequently no theorem valid for
every fixed system in the class of \textup{(A1)} can replace $O_U(m)$ by
$o(m)$.  This is sharpness in growth order; it does not assert that the
computed $C_U$ is numerically optimal or that coefficient one is universal.
To the authors' knowledge, this is the first aperture-linear inverse-depth
bound for cyclic language-rank recodings over fixed Pisot numeration systems.

Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient} then frees the contraction
from the code's canonical difference alphabet.  For an arbitrary fixed
$D\ge1$, it treats the finite overlap graph whose edges are bounded
$U$-representations of zero with coefficients in $[-D,D]$ and a uniformly
bounded terminal quotient coefficient.  Either a first-coordinate-nonzero
state reaches a directed cycle, or every path from such a state has fewer
than $C(U,D)m$ edges.  This is a quantitative finite-state theorem about the
following narrow crossing: the rows are classical bounded
$U$-representations of zero, and the aperture-dependent overlap transient is
the new quantity.  Its statement uses no
canonical rank, legal-word alphabet, cyclic reduction, digit-difference
realization, image shift, or inverse decoder.  The fixed cubic words from
\textup{(A3)}, read only as coefficient paths, show that the linear order is
sharp already for $D=1$.

The adjective ``standard'' is avoided here because it normally specifies the
initial conditions $u_{-1}=\cdots=u_{-d+1}=0$, $u_0=1$.  We allow
nonstandard initial values.  Some iconic systems with standard initial
conditions begin $1,1,\ldots$ and enter the strictly increasing class only
after the familiar shift to $1,2,\ldots$.  Condition~F would also be a real
restriction: the principal root of $x^3-3x^2+2x-1$ is Pisot without
Condition~F \cite[Theorem~3]{Akiyama2000}.

The construction must be separated from numerical $\beta$-normalization.
Our congruences compare integer language ranks, not values in
$\mathbb Z[\beta]$.  For example, if $\beta^2-3\beta+1=0$, then
$(Q_0,Q_1,Q_2)=(1,3,8)$ and the rank of $(2,2)$ is $8$, so its cyclic image
is $(0,0)$; no equality $2+2\beta=0$ is asserted.  The theorem is about this
cyclic language-rank recoding, not numerical $\beta$-normalization in general,
arbitrary $\beta$-transformations, or all Pisot symbolic dynamics.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Priority comparison.}
The closest precedents do not subsume \textup{(A1)} for distinct quantitative
reasons.  Frougny's finite-automaton normalization and addition results and
the Berend--Frougny characterization establish finite-state computability in
Pisot bases, not an aperture-linear optimal inverse window for a prescribed
cyclic rank map \cite{Frougny1992,BerendFrougny1994}.  Regularity of bounded
zero-representation automata is classical, including for Pisot systems
without Condition~F, but regularity alone does not bound the survival time of
one-position overlap chains by $C(U,D)m$
\cite{Frougny1992,CartonSudberyYassawi2026}.  Carton--Sudbery--Yassawi's
equivalence of zero preservation and Condition~F is the closest
methodological precedent: it uses Pisot contraction on shifted bounded zero
representations, but has no cyclic rank map, collision graph, future-only
inverse, or $O(m)$ survival-depth theorem
\cite{CartonSudberyYassawi2026}.  Ashley's linear-bound papers are the most
likely source of a mistaken priority objection: their bounds concern
encoders into constrained systems and are linear in the number of states of
a presenting graph (with the relevant memory and anticipation parameters),
whereas the natural pair/difference presentations here have exponentially
many aperture-$m$ states; a state-count-linear theorem would therefore still
be exponential in $m$ \cite{Ashley1988,Ashley1996}.  The
Frougny--Sakarovitch bounded-delay theorem converts a rational relation already
known to have bounded delay into a synchronized, letter-to-letter form; it
does not deduce $O(m)$ delay from injectivity
\cite{FrougnySakarovitch1991}.  Finally, classical pair and fiber graphs give
reachable-cycle injectivity tests and finite inverse windows, but their
generic difference-state count is $(2d_U+1)^{m-1}-1$, not the new
aperture-linear estimate
\cite{AmorosoPatt1972,Richardson1972,Nasu1977,Head1989,LindMarcus1995,
Nasu1995}.  These precedents supply the automata and collision frameworks;
none gives the quantitative implication \textup{(A1)}.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Quadratic and simple-Parry results.}
For every quadratic Pisot base, Theorem
\ref{thm:full-quadratic-pisot-threshold} classifies the least nontrivial
aperture $m_*(\beta)$:
\[
m_*(\beta)=3
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\operatorname{minpoly}(\beta)\in
\{x^2-ax-a:a\ge1\}\cup\{x^2-ax+1:a\ge3\},
\]
and $m_*(\beta)=2$ otherwise.  At and above threshold, finite-block
injectivity begins at length $m$, the natural overlap presentation is the
right Fischer cover with $(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)^{m-1}$ states, and the
image is exactly $m$-step, not $(m-1)$-step.  These are exact conclusions for
the cyclic rank map, using standard Fischer--Krieger theory only as the
presentation framework \cite{Fischer1975,Krieger1984,LindMarcus1995}.

For arbitrary nonintegral simple-Parry bases, without a Pisot hypothesis,
Theorem~\ref{thm:simple-parry-causal-collision} quotients the classical pair
graph by coordinatewise differences and obtains an exact longest-path formula
for $\ell_{\rm cau}$.  The unique predecessor of the zero state upgrades the
injective case to a future-only inverse.  Applied to Bassino's cubic
simple-Parry Pisot family \cite{Bassino2002}, this gives injective apertures
$m_n=n-1$ with exact depth $n-1$, a complementary varying-system
unboundedness calculation.

The paper is organized with the structural result first.  Section
\ref{sec:pisot-linear-depth} proves \textup{(A1)}--\textup{(A3)} and the
arbitrary-alphabet bounded-zero theorem.  Sections
\ref{sec:stabilization}--\ref{sec:metallic-cover} develop the Fibonacci and
metallic prototypes.  Section~\ref{sec:quadratic-pisot-extension} proves the
quadratic classification and exact local structure.  Section
\ref{sec:simple-parry-causal-obstructions} develops the arithmetic difference
quotient and the cubic varying-system theorem.  Supplementary material holds
the entropy, Blackwell, pressure, fiber, discrepancy, and geometric
consequences, none of which enters the parameter-uniform proof.


 succeeded in 237ms:
\begin{corollary}[Global overlap dichotomy for bounded zero representations]
\label{cor:pisot-zero-strip-synchronization}
For a fixed strictly increasing Pisot numeration $U$ as in the theorem and an
aperture $m\ge2$,
consider all bounded zero words of length $m+1$ of the form
\[
[e_t,\ldots,e_{t+m-1},-k_t]_U=0
\]
which overlap one digit at a time.  Each row is a standard bounded
$U$-representation of zero; only its organization into this one-position
overlap chain is specific here.  Exactly one of the following global
alternatives holds: some chain with a nonzero coefficient has a finite prefix
followed by a periodic bounded zero representation; or every nonzero chain
has fewer than $(C_U+1)m$ rows.  The latter bound has sharp order $\Theta(m)$
for $U_\theta$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The overlapping rows are exactly paths in $\Gamma_{U,m}$ by
\eqref{eq:pisot-zero-word-bridge}.  A cycle reachable from $B_{U,m}$ gives
the first alternative by repeating the cycle.  If no such cycle is
reachable, \eqref{eq:pisot-linear-A1} bounds every path from $B_{U,m}$.
Unless a chain is zero, at most $m-1$ initial shifts pass before one of its
nonzero coordinates occupies the first position, after which fewer than
$C_Um$ further rows are possible.  The two alternatives are mutually
exclusive because the periodic tail makes chains arbitrarily long.  The
words $E_m$ in \eqref{eq:pisot-linear-A3} give the matching lower order for
$U_\theta$.
\end{proof}

We now detach the contraction argument from the canonical digit-difference
alphabet.  Fix $D\ge1$ and retain the effective Binet and growth data in
\eqref{eq:pisot-binet}--\eqref{eq:pisot-effective-growth}.  Put
\begin{equation}
K_{U,D}=\left\lceil\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}\right\rceil
\label{eq:bounded-zero-carry-bound}
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}
\delta_{U,D}=\min\left\{|a+k-\beta l|:
 |a|\le D,\ |k|,|l|\le K_{U,D},\ a+k-\beta l\ne0\right\}.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-separation}
\end{equation}
The variables in this minimum are integers.  Since $\beta$ is irrational,
$\delta_{U,D}>0$, and exact algebraic-number comparison computes it.  Define
\begin{align}
M_{U,D}&=\frac1{c_0}\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}|c_i|
 \left(\frac{D}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_{U,D}\right),\nonumber\\
B_{U,D}&=D+(1+\beta)M_{U,D},
\label{eq:bounded-zero-conjugate-bound}
\end{align}
and
\begin{equation}
m_0(U,D)=\min\{m\ge2:\beta^m\delta_{U,D}>B_{U,D}\}.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-contraction-threshold}
\end{equation}
All these constants are effective from $U$ and $D$.

\begin{lemma}[Adjacent collapse for an arbitrary bounded coefficient alphabet]
\label{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}
Let $U$ satisfy the integral recurrence whose characteristic polynomial is
the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$, and fix
$D\ge1$.  If $m\ge m_0(U,D)$, coefficients
$e_t,\ldots,e_{t+m}\in[-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z$, and integers $k_t,k_{t+1}$
satisfy
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}=k_tu_m,
\qquad
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+1+j}=k_{t+1}u_m,
\]
then
\[
k_{t+1}=0,\qquad e_{t+m}=-k_t.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The growth bounds give, for either row,
\[
|k_t|\le D\frac{\sum_{j<m}u_j}{u_m}
 <\frac{DA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}\le K_{U,D},
\]
and similarly $|k_{t+1}|\le K_{U,D}$.  For every embedding set
\[
A_{t,i}=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}e_{t+j}\beta_i^j-k_t\beta_i^m.
\]
The recurrence and its Binet expansion give $\sum_i c_iA_{t,i}=0$.
For $i\ge1$,
\[
|A_{t,i}|\le\frac{D}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_{U,D},
\]
so $|A_{t,0}|\le M_{U,D}$, and the same holds at $t+1$.
Direct subtraction of the adjacent dominant-embedding sums gives the exact
identity
\begin{equation}
\beta^m(e_{t+m}+k_t-\beta k_{t+1})
=\beta A_{t+1,0}-A_{t,0}+e_t.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}
\end{equation}
The right side has modulus at most $B_{U,D}$.  If the parenthesis on the
left were nonzero, \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-separation} and
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-contraction-threshold} would make the left side
strictly larger than $B_{U,D}$, a contradiction.  Hence
$e_{t+m}+k_t=\beta k_{t+1}$.  Irrationality of $\beta$ and integrality of
the three coefficients force $k_{t+1}=0$ and $e_{t+m}=-k_t$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Linear transient bound for bounded $U$-representations of zero]
\label{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
Let $U=(u_n)_{n\ge0}$ be a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
with $u_0=1$, satisfying the integral recurrence whose characteristic
polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a nonintegral Pisot number $\beta$.
Fix $D\ge1$.  For $m\ge2$, let $G_m(U,D)$ be the directed graph with vertex
set
\[
([-D,D]\cap\mathbb Z)^{m-1}.
\]
There is an edge
\[
(e_0,\ldots,e_{m-2})\longrightarrow
(e_1,\ldots,e_{m-2},e_{m-1})
\]
if and only if there is an integer $c$ such that
\begin{equation}
e_0u_0+e_1u_1+\cdots+e_{m-1}u_{m-1}+cu_m=0.
\label{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}
\end{equation}
The completing coefficient is unique and necessarily satisfies
$|c|\le K_{U,D}$.
Then there is an effectively computable $C(U,D)<\infty$ such that, for every
$m\ge2$, if no directed cycle of $G_m(U,D)$ is reachable from a vertex whose
first coordinate is nonzero, every directed path beginning at such a vertex
has fewer than $C(U,D)m$ edges.

Equivalently, after shifting a chain so that its initial exposed coefficient
is nonzero, either it reaches a directed cycle, or it has fewer than
$C(U,D)m$ further overlaps.  The graphs are finite-state recognizers of these
overlap chains, and their edges are bounded $U$-representations of zero by
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.

The linear order is sharp.  For the fixed recurrence
\[
u_0=1,\quad u_1=2,\quad u_2=4,\qquad
u_{n+3}=2u_{n+2}-u_{n+1}+u_n,
\]
whose leading root is the Pisot root of $x^3-2x^2+x-1$, and for $D=1$, the
maximum finite path length is at least $m-3$ for every $m\ge4$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For an edge at time $t$, write $k_t=-c$ in
\eqref{eq:bounded-zero-overlap-edge}.  The growth estimate gives
$|k_t|\le K_{U,D}$ uniformly in $m$.  Suppose first that
$m\ge m_0(U,D)$.  Along any path of at least two edges, Lemma
\ref{lem:bounded-zero-arbitrary-alphabet-collapse}, applied to each adjacent
pair, gives
\[
k_1=k_2=\cdots=0,\qquad
e_m=-k_0,\qquad e_{m+1}=e_{m+2}=\cdots=0.
\]
A path of $m+1$ edges therefore reaches the all-zero vertex.  That vertex
has a loop, so such a path would make a directed cycle reachable from its
initial vertex.  Under the hypothesis of the theorem, every path has at
most $m$ edges for $m\ge m_0(U,D)$.

For each of the finitely many $2\le m<m_0(U,D)$, the graph is finite.  When
no cycle is reachable from the first-coordinate-nonzero vertices, exact
acyclic longest-path search computes the maximum $p_{U,D}(m)$; set it to
zero otherwise.  Thus, for example,
\[
C(U,D)=\max\left(2,
 \max_{2\le m<m_0(U,D)}
 \left\lceil\frac{1+p_{U,D}(m)}m\right\rceil\right)
\]
is effective and has the required property, with the inner maximum understood
as zero when $m_0(U,D)=2$.

For sharpness, the coefficient words $E_m$ in
\eqref{eq:theta-terminal-word} satisfy the integer recurrence relations
classified in \eqref{eq:theta-suffix-renormalization}--
\eqref{eq:theta-five-tail-table}.  Read only as coefficient words, they give
paths in $G_m(U,1)$ of length
$2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-2\ge m-3$.  The next bounded-coefficient path set is
empty by the same exact terminal calculation, so no reachable cycle occurs.
This conclusion uses the recurrence identities alone, independently of any
realization of the coefficients as differences of raw words.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Logical boundary of the standard-object theorem]
\label{rem:bounded-zero-standard-object-boundary}
The statement and proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded-zero-linear-transient}
use only a fixed Pisot recurrence, bounded integer coefficients, the
distinguished weight $u_m$, and one-position overlaps of bounded
$U$-representations of zero.  They use no canonical rank bijection, legal-word
alphabet, cyclic reduction map, positive/negative realization of
coefficients, image shift, or inverse decoder.  The distinguished modulus
$u_m$ and consecutive recurrence weights remain essential: replacing $u_m$
by an unrelated modulus destroys \eqref{eq:bounded-zero-adjacent-identity}.
The theorem therefore does not concern numerical $\beta$-normalization,
successor carry propagation, general synchronized transductions, general
right-closing maps, or a broad class of sliding block codes.
\end{remark}

 succeeded in 251ms:
\section{Fixed-base linear depth for Pisot numeration}
\label{sec:pisot-linear-depth}

We begin with the fixed-system theorem.  A Pisot linear numeration system is
a sequence $U=(u_j)_{j\ge0}$ of positive integers
which satisfies the integral recurrence whose characteristic polynomial is
the minimal polynomial of a Pisot number; the initial values need not be the
standard ones.  Here ``standard'' has its technical meaning
$u_{-1}=\cdots=u_{-d+1}=0$, $u_0=1$; we do not use it as a synonym for the
larger class considered below.  We assume throughout this section that $U$
is strictly increasing and $u_0=1$.  Let
\[
D_U=\{0,\ldots,d_U\}
\]
be its canonical greedy digit set.  The canonical greedy $U$-normal words of
length $m$ are
\begin{equation}
X_{U,m}=\left\{x\in D_U^m:
 \sum_{j=0}^k x_ju_j<u_{k+1}\quad(0\le k<m)\right\}.
\label{eq:pisot-canonical-words}
\end{equation}
Thus
\[
\Val_{U,m}(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}x_ju_j
\]
maps $X_{U,m}$ bijectively onto $\{0,\ldots,u_m-1\}$.  Define
\begin{equation}
\Fold_{U,m}(w)=
(\Val_{U,m}|_{X_{U,m}})^{-1}
       (\Val_{U,m}(w)\bmod u_m)
\label{eq:pisot-canonical-fold}
\end{equation}
and let $\Phi_{U,m}$ be the corresponding sliding code on
$D_U^{\mathbb Z}$.  Define $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)$ to be the least $L\ge1$ for
which the raw digit at time zero is determined, on the image of
$\Phi_{U,m}$, by the output windows at times $0,\ldots,L-1$; set it to
$\infty$ if no such $L$ exists.  Thus $\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)-1$ is the least
anticipation of an inverse constrained to have memory zero.

If each output word is relabeled by its canonical rank in
$\mathbb Z/u_m\mathbb Z$, the local rule is
\[
(x_0,\ldots,x_{m-1})\longmapsto
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_jx_j\pmod {u_m}.
\]
Thus the word-valued rule is a symbolwise bijective relabeling of a modular
weighted sliding block code.  The weights, the modulus $u_m$, and their
variation with $m$ are specific to the present family.  This is not a linear
cellular automaton: in general $D_U$ is only a digit subset of
$\mathbb Z/u_m\mathbb Z$, not a module on which the rule is a group
endomorphism.

The scope is not a disguised standard-initial-value convention.  For
example, standard $k$-bonacci initial conditions begin $1,1,\ldots$ and fail
strict increase, whereas the customary shift beginning $1,2,\ldots$ belongs
to the present class.  Nor do we assume Condition~F: that would be a genuine
restriction, since the principal root of $x^3-3x^2+2x-1$ is Pisot but does
not satisfy Condition~F \cite[Theorem~3]{Akiyama2000}.  The equivalence of
Condition~F with zero preservation is proved in
\cite{CartonSudberyYassawi2026}, but neither property is used below.

Put $\Delta_U=[-d_U,d_U]\cap\mathbb Z$ and, for $L\ge1$, set
\begin{equation}
\mathcal N_{U,m,L}=\left\{e\in\Delta_U^{m+L-1}:
 e_0\ne0,\quad
 \sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}\equiv0\pmod {u_m}
 \ (0\le t<L)\right\}.
\label{eq:pisot-bad-sliding-set}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, $\mathcal N_{U,m,L}$ is the finite-block kernel of the
$L$-row banded Toeplitz operator with kernel
$(u_0,\ldots,u_{m-1})$ over $\mathbb Z/u_m\mathbb Z$, restricted by
$e_0\ne0$ and by the coefficient bound $\Delta_U$.
Every row in this system has a uniquely defined carry $k_t\in\mathbb Z$:
\begin{equation}
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_je_{t+j}=k_tu_m.
\label{eq:pisot-window-carry}
\end{equation}
Equivalently,
\begin{equation}
[e_t,e_{t+1},\ldots,e_{t+m-1},-k_t]_U=0,
\label{eq:pisot-zero-word-bridge}
\end{equation}
where the last coordinate has weight $u_m$.  This is the promised bridge to
the standard language of bounded zero representations; it is not an
identification with numerical $\beta$-normalization.

\begin{lemma}[Exact collision quotient for $U$]
\label{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient}
For every $m\ge2$,
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)
=\min\{L\ge1:\mathcal N_{U,m,L}=\varnothing\}.
\label{eq:pisot-exact-depth}
\end{equation}
Let $\Gamma_{U,m}$ have vertex set $\Delta_U^{m-1}$ and an edge
$(v_0,\ldots,v_{m-2})\to(v_1,\ldots,v_{m-2},a)$ exactly when
\[
\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}u_jv_j+u_{m-1}a\equiv0\pmod {u_m}.
\]
If $B_{U,m}$ is the set of vertices with first coordinate nonzero, then
\begin{equation}
\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)=1+\sup\{\text{edge length of a path from }B_{U,m}\}.
\label{eq:pisot-longest-path}
\end{equation}
Moreover, $\Phi_{U,m}$ is injective if and only if no directed cycle of
$\Gamma_{U,m}$ is reachable from $B_{U,m}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Two folded windows agree exactly when the difference of their raw values is
divisible by $u_m$, because the canonical normal words represent
$0,\ldots,u_m-1$ bijectively.  Every $e\in\Delta_U$ is realized as a
difference of two digits by taking its positive and negative parts in
$D_U$.  Hence two output $L$-blocks with distinct initial raw digits are
equivalent to a vector in $\mathcal N_{U,m,L}$, with no lost collisions.
Reading consecutive difference windows gives precisely the paths from
$B_{U,m}$ in $\Gamma_{U,m}$, proving \eqref{eq:pisot-exact-depth} and
\eqref{eq:pisot-longest-path}.  A reachable nonzero cycle can be repeated
periodically and is realized by two raw points.

It remains only to note why injectivity rules out the zero loop as a
reachable cycle.  If $d_U\ge u_m$, the constant nonzero difference sequence
$e_t=u_m$ belongs to $\Delta_U^{\mathbb Z}$ and every window sum is divisible
by $u_m$; hence $\Phi_{U,m}$ is noninjective and the difference graph has a
nonzero loop.  In the only remaining case, $d_U<u_m$.  If a vertex has an
edge to the zero vertex, its overlap coordinates vanish and its first
coordinate $a$ satisfies $a\equiv0\pmod {u_m}$.  The bound $|a|\le d_U<u_m$
then gives $a=0$, so the zero vertex is its own unique predecessor.  This
proves the last assertion without imposing a restriction on the allowed
nonstandard initial values.
\end{proof}

We next make the contraction constant explicit.  Let
$\beta=\beta_0,\beta_1,\ldots,\beta_{s-1}$ be the roots of the minimal
polynomial, with $\beta>1$ and $|\beta_i|<1$ for $i\ge1$.  The recurrence and
the initial values effectively give algebraic numbers $c_i$ such that
\begin{equation}
u_n=\sum_{i=0}^{s-1}c_i\beta_i^n,
\qquad c_0>0.
\label{eq:pisot-binet}
\end{equation}
Effective root isolation gives positive algebraic bounds $b_U,A_U$ with
\begin{equation}
b_U\beta^n\le u_n\le A_U\beta^n\qquad(n\ge0).
\label{eq:pisot-effective-growth}
\end{equation}
Indeed, isolate a point after which the total conjugate contribution in
\eqref{eq:pisot-binet} is at most $c_0\beta^n/2$, and inspect the remaining
finite prefix.

Set
\begin{equation}
K_U^{*}=
\left\lceil\frac{d_UA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}\right\rceil.
\label{eq:pisot-analytic-carry-bound}
\end{equation}
Equation \eqref{eq:pisot-effective-growth} shows directly, without a
normalization automaton or a zero-preservation hypothesis, that every carry
in \eqref{eq:pisot-window-carry} lies in the integer interval
$[-K_U^{*},K_U^{*}]$, since
\[
|k_t|\le d_U\frac{\sum_{j<m}u_j}{u_m}
<\frac{d_UA_U}{b_U(\beta-1)}.
\]

For nonintegral $\beta$, define the effective positive separation
\begin{equation}
\delta_U^{*}=\min\left\{|a+k-\beta l|:
 a\in\Delta_U,\ |k|,|l|\le K_U^{*},\ a+k-\beta l\ne0\right\}
\label{eq:pisot-effective-separation}
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}
M_U=\frac1{c_0}\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}|c_i|
 \left(\frac{d_U}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_U^{*}\right),
\qquad
B_U=d_U+(1+\beta)M_U.
\label{eq:pisot-conjugate-bound}
\end{equation}
The minimum in \eqref{eq:pisot-effective-separation} is positive: if
$l\ne0$, irrationality of $\beta$ prevents equality with the integer
$a+k$, while for $l=0$ every nonzero value has modulus at least one.  All
comparisons are comparisons of algebraic numbers and are effective.
Finally let
\begin{equation}
m_U=\min\{m\ge2:\beta^m\delta_U^{*}>B_U\}.
\label{eq:pisot-contraction-threshold}
\end{equation}

For a fixed $U$, the classical finite automaton recognizing bounded zero
representations over a fixed alphabet may be used to discard interval values
that never occur \cite{Frougny1992,CartonSudberyYassawi2026}.  This can sharpen
the numerical value of $C_U$ below, but it is neither a hypothesis nor a
logical input to the proof.

\begin{lemma}[Adjacent-window Pisot contraction]
\label{lem:pisot-adjacent-window-contraction}
Suppose that $\beta$ is nonintegral, $m\ge m_U$, and two consecutive rows
of \eqref{eq:pisot-window-carry} hold.  Then
\begin{equation}
k_{t+1}=0,\qquad e_{t+m}=-k_t.
\label{eq:pisot-carry-collapse}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For each embedding define
\[
A_{t,i}=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}e_{t+j}\beta_i^j-k_t\beta_i^m.
\]
Substitution of \eqref{eq:pisot-binet} into
\eqref{eq:pisot-window-carry} gives
$\sum_i c_iA_{t,i}=0$.  For $i\ge1$,
\[
 |A_{t,i}|\le \frac{d_U}{1-|\beta_i|}+K_U^{*},
\]
and therefore $|A_{t,0}|\le M_U$.  Direct subtraction of two adjacent
dominant-embedding sums yields
\begin{equation}
\beta^m(e_{t+m}+k_t-\beta k_{t+1})
=\beta A_{t+1,0}-A_{t,0}+e_t.
\label{eq:pisot-adjacent-binet-identity}
\end{equation}
The right side has modulus at most $B_U$.  By
\eqref{eq:pisot-effective-separation} and
\eqref{eq:pisot-contraction-threshold}, the parenthesis on the left must
vanish.  Since $e_{t+m}+k_t$ and $k_{t+1}$ are integers and $\beta$ is
irrational, this is possible only in \eqref{eq:pisot-carry-collapse}.
\end{proof}

In the degree-one case set $C_U=1$.  Otherwise, for $2\le m<m_U$, let
$p_U(m)$ be the maximum edge length of a path from $B_{U,m}$ when no directed
cycle is reachable from $B_{U,m}$, and put $p_U(m)=0$ otherwise.  This finite
list is computed by exact acyclic longest-path searches.  Define
\begin{equation}
C_U=\max\left(2,
 \max_{2\le m<m_U}\left\lceil\frac{1+p_U(m)}m\right\rceil\right).
\label{eq:effective-linear-constant}
\end{equation}
Here and below the inner maximum is zero if $m_U=2$.
This is a finite, effective recipe from the recurrence and the canonical
digit set.  The large-aperture part
of the recipe is the Pisot contraction estimate, not a count of the
$(2d_U+1)^{m-1}$ difference vertices.

\begin{theorem}[Fixed-base linear causal depth and sharpness for Pisot numeration]
\label{thm:pisot-linear-depth-sharpness}
Let $U$ be any strictly increasing Pisot numeration system with $u_0=1$,
canonical greedy digit set and canonical normal words.  Then there is an
effectively computable $C_U<\infty$ such that, for every $m\ge2$,
\begin{equation*}
\Phi_{U,m}\text{ injective}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le C_Um.
\tag{A1}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A1}
\end{equation*}
Equivalently, if no first-coordinate-nonzero state of $\Gamma_{U,m}$ can
reach a directed cycle, every directed path from that initial state set has
length at most $C_Um-1$.

Let $\theta$ be the real root of
\[
x^3-2x^2+x-1
\]
and let $U_\theta=(Q_j(\theta))_{j\ge0}$ be given by
\begin{equation}
Q_0(\theta)=1,\quad Q_1(\theta)=2,\quad Q_2(\theta)=4,
\quad Q_{j+3}(\theta)=2Q_{j+2}(\theta)-Q_{j+1}(\theta)+Q_j(\theta).
\label{eq:theta-count-recurrence}
\end{equation}
For every $m\ge4$,
\begin{equation*}
\Phi_{\theta,m}\text{ is injective},\qquad
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\left\lfloor\frac m2\right\rfloor-1.
\tag{A2}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A2}
\end{equation*}
More precisely, if
$\lambda_m=2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-1$, then
\begin{equation*}
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}=\{E_m,-E_m\}\ne\varnothing,
\qquad
\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}=\varnothing,
\tag{A3}\label{eq:pisot-linear-A3}
\end{equation*}
where, writing concatenation by juxtaposition and changing every sign by a
prefixed minus,
\begin{equation}
\begin{gathered}
E_4=(1,-1,-1,-1,1),\\
E_m=(1,0,-E_{m-2},0,0)\quad(m\ge6\text{ even}),\\
E_m=(E_{m-1},0)\quad(m\ge5\text{ odd}).
\end{gathered}
\label{eq:theta-terminal-word}
\end{equation}
Consequently
\[
\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)}m=1,
\]
so no theorem valid for every fixed system in the class of \textup{(A1)} can
replace $O_U(m)$ by $o(m)$.  This does not assert that the computed $C_U$ is
numerically optimal or that coefficient one is universal across systems.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We first prove \eqref{eq:pisot-linear-A1}.  The degree-one case is
$u_j=b^j$, $D_U=\{0,\ldots,b-1\}$; its fold is the identity and
$\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)=1$.  Suppose that $\beta$ is nonintegral and
$m\ge m_U$.  If a vector in $\mathcal N_{U,m,L}$ has $L\ge2$, apply
Lemma~\ref{lem:pisot-adjacent-window-contraction} for
$0\le t\le L-2$.  It gives
\[
k_1=\cdots=k_{L-1}=0,\qquad
e_m=-k_0,\qquad e_{m+1}=\cdots=e_{m+L-2}=0.
\]
If $L=m+1$, the terminal difference vertex is therefore zero.  It cannot
be reached from $B_{U,m}$ when no directed cycle is reachable, because the
zero vertex has a loop.  Hence every such path has at most $m$ edges and
$\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le m+1\le2m$.  The finitely many smaller apertures are
covered by the definition of $C_U$.  Lemma
\ref{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient} gives both formulations of
\eqref{eq:pisot-linear-A1}.

We turn to $\theta$.  The polynomial is negative at $7/4$, positive at
$351/200$, and has only one real zero; the product of the two nonreal roots
has modulus $1/\theta$.  Thus $\theta$ is Pisot.  Its defining equation
gives
\[
1=\theta^{-1}+\theta^{-2}+\theta^{-4},
\]
and every proper suffix of $1101\,0^\infty$ is lexicographically smaller.
Parry's criterion therefore gives $d_\theta(1)=1101\,0^\infty$, and the
canonical language counts are exactly \eqref{eq:theta-count-recurrence}.
In particular, the digit and difference alphabets are $\{0,1\}$ and
$\{-1,0,1\}$.  The recurrence also implies the positive recurrence
$Q_{n+4}=Q_{n+3}+Q_{n+2}+Q_n$.  Thus
$2Q_{n+1}-3Q_n>0$ follows from its four positive initial values.  Starting
with $Q_0<2Q_1$ and using
$\sum_{j<m+1}Q_j<3Q_m<2Q_{m+1}$ then gives
\begin{equation}
\sum_{j<m}Q_j(\theta)<2Q_m(\theta),
\label{eq:theta-carry-one}
\end{equation}
so every window carry belongs to $\{-1,0,1\}$.

For completeness, we verify the contraction threshold rather than invoking
an unspecified asymptotic.  If $\rho,\overline\rho$ are the nonreal roots,
write
\[
Q_n=c_\theta\theta^n+c_\rho\rho^n+\overline{c_\rho}\,\overline\rho^n.
\]
The rational isolating interval above and the residue formula for the
generating function
\[
\sum_{n\ge0}Q_nz^n=\frac{1+z^2}{1-2z+z^2-z^3}
\]
give
\[
1.26<c_\theta<1.27,\qquad |c_\rho|<0.19,\qquad |\rho|<0.756.
\]
Here $c_\alpha=(\alpha^2+1)/(3\alpha^2-4\alpha+1)$ for every root
$\alpha$, and $|\rho|=\theta^{-1/2}$.  Thus all displayed decimals are
terminating rational bounds obtained by substitution of the endpoints
$7/4$ and $351/200$ and exact rational arithmetic.
Hence the dominant-embedding error in the proof of Lemma
\ref{lem:pisot-adjacent-window-contraction} is less than $1.54$.  Here
$\delta_\theta=2-\theta>0.245$, and
\[
\theta^6\delta_\theta>7.03
>1+(1+\theta)1.54.
\]
It follows that \eqref{eq:pisot-carry-collapse} holds for every $m\ge6$.
The apertures $m=4,5$ will be included in the exact terminal check below.

It remains to classify the longest paths.  Fix $m\ge6$ and a vector with
at least two collision rows.  After the carry collapse, all its coordinates
beyond $m$ vanish except possibly $e_m=-k_0$.  Define the full suffix sums
\[
R_t=\sum_{j\ge0}Q_j(\theta)e_{t+j}.
\]
Every collision row with $t\ge1$ is now $R_t=0$, and the first row together
with $e_m=-k_0$ gives $R_0=0$.  The recurrence implies the exact identity
\begin{equation}
R_t=e_t+e_{t+2}+2R_{t+1}-R_{t+2}+R_{t+3}.
\label{eq:theta-suffix-renormalization}
\end{equation}
Thus four consecutive zero suffix sums force $e_t=-e_{t+2}$.

Let first $m$ be even and take $L=m-2=\lambda_m-1$.  We have
$R_t=0$ for $0\le t\le m-3$.  The terminal equation is
\[
e_{m-3}+2e_{m-2}+4e_{m-1}+7e_m=0.
\]
In $\{-1,0,1\}^4$ its complete solution set is
\begin{equation}
(0,0,0,0),\qquad \pm(1,1,1,-1).
\label{eq:theta-four-tail-table}
\end{equation}
The zero tail propagates backwards to the zero word.  From a nonzero tail,
the equations $R_{m-4}=R_{m-5}=0$ give respectively
$e_{m-4}=-e_{m-3}$ and $e_{m-5}=0$; then
\eqref{eq:theta-suffix-renormalization} propagates the alternating pair
uniquely to the left.  This is precisely the recursion
\eqref{eq:theta-terminal-word}, and both signs occur.  Hence the first set
in \eqref{eq:pisot-linear-A3} is $\{\pm E_m\}$.  With one more row, the
terminal equation is
\[
e_{m-2}+2e_{m-1}+4e_m=0,
\]
whose only solution in $\{-1,0,1\}^3$ is zero.  Backward propagation then
contradicts $e_0\ne0$.  Thus $\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}$ is empty.

Now let $m$ be odd and take $L=m-3=\lambda_m-1$.  The terminal five-digit
equation has, up to sign, exactly the following solutions:
\begin{equation}
(0,0,0,0,0),\quad
(1,1,1,-1,0),\quad
(1,0,1,1,-1),\quad
(1,-1,-1,-1,1).
\label{eq:theta-five-tail-table}
\end{equation}
Successive solution of $R_{m-5}=0$ gives respectively
$0,-1,0,0$ for the preceding digit.  Since $m-5$ is even,
\eqref{eq:theta-suffix-renormalization} propagates this digit to $e_0$ up
to sign.  The zero tail and the last two nonzero tails therefore give
$e_0=0$; only $\pm(1,1,1,-1,0)$ survive.  They give exactly the even
terminal word for aperture $m-1$ followed by zero, namely $\pm E_m$.
With one more row the terminal four-digit alternatives are
\eqref{eq:theta-four-tail-table}; now $m-5$ has even parity and its forced
zero propagates to $e_0$.  Hence the next obstruction set is empty.

For $m=4,5$, successive exact division in the two or three window
congruences gives
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
m&Q_m&\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m-1}
&\mathcal N_{\theta,m,\lambda_m}\\ \hline
4&12&\{\pm(1,-1,-1,-1,1)\}&\varnothing\\
5&21&\{\pm(1,-1,-1,-1,1,0)\}&\varnothing.
\end{array}
\]
This is an exhaustive integer calculation: in each row the quotient is in
$\{-1,0,1\}$ by \eqref{eq:theta-carry-one}, and the last digit determines
the preceding bounded digit successively.  Thus \eqref{eq:pisot-linear-A3}
holds for every $m\ge4$.  Lemma
\ref{lem:pisot-exact-collision-quotient} now gives
\eqref{eq:pisot-linear-A2}, including injectivity, and the limiting statement
is immediate.
\end{proof}

\section*{Data and code availability}

No external data sets were used.  The proofs in the paper are analytic; the
finite computations supplied with the source are regression tests and are not
certificates of any parameter-uniform theorem.

The accompanying source archive contains the following scripts and saved
text output:
\begin{flushleft}
\path{artifacts/verify_metallic_threshold.py},\\
\path{artifacts/verify_quadratic_pisot_threshold.py},\\
\path{artifacts/test_quadratic_pisot_threshold.py},\\
\path{artifacts/verify_simple_parry_causal.py},\\
\path{artifacts/test_verify_simple_parry_causal.py},\\
and \path{artifacts/test_bounded_zero_arbitrary_D.py}.
\end{flushleft}
The quadratic rank battery tests lengths
$1\le m\le5$ for the negative chamber
$1\le a\le6$, $1\le b\le a$, and for the positive chamber
$3\le a\le8$, $1\le b\le a-2$.  The block-language battery tests
$m=n\in\{2,3,4\}$ on the same 42 parameter pairs.  Additional tests use
$n=m+1$ for six boundary/interior representatives through $m=4$, and periodic
counts through period six for four extremal representatives.
The extended local-structure battery checks all 15 dual chamber pairs with
$3\le q\le7$ at apertures two and three, the exact future-only inverse lengths and
finite-block onset through $m=5$ for four representatives, and the critical
periodic-fiber histogram through period six for four extremal representatives.
The simple-Parry battery checks the Parry recurrences and consecutive colex
ranks on five finite Parry words through length six, compares Toeplitz bad
blocks with collision-graph path counts in three systems, exhausts the
$p$-bonacci future-only calculation for $3\le p\le8$, and enumerates all bad
blocks through four outputs for the cubic word $101$ at aperture four.  It
also checks the explicit period-one and period-four collision witnesses at
apertures two and three for that cubic base.  The extended graph battery
checks the unique zero predecessor, injectivity--future-only-invertibility equivalence,
state-count bound, and bounded periodic witnesses in three representative
systems.  It also checks all three aperture-two regimes and the quadratic
specializations on five finite Parry words.
The arbitrary-coefficient bounded-zero battery exhausts 12 adjacent-window
cases for the non-Condition-F root of $x^3-3x^2+2x-1$ with $D=2,3$.
It checks both the canonical count recurrence and the nonstandard initial
values $(1,2,4)$, including the first two apertures at and beyond the latter
$D=3$ contraction threshold.  These are falsification tests, not inputs to
the contraction proof.
The metallic
script tests the main cases $1\le A\le4$, $2\le m\le5$, together with
$A\in\{5,6\}$, $2\le m\le4$; its extended block tests use $A=1$,
$3\le m\le5$, together with $2\le A\le4$, $2\le m\le4$.  It also checks
the golden block-bijection identity for $3\le m\le6$ at $n=m,m+1$ and
periodic counts through period six for $1\le A\le4$ at $m=2$.

These internal paths identify files in the review bundle only; they are not
presented as persistent URLs.  A versioned public archival release and its DOI
have not yet been assigned and must be supplied before final publication.

\begin{abstract}
We study the symbolic dynamics of cyclic recodings of greedy Pisot
numeration systems.  The canonical rank of a legal length-$m$ word is
extended to the full digit cube, reduced modulo the number $u_m$ of legal
words, and overlapped along consecutive windows.  This is a congruence of
integer language ranks, not numerical $\beta$-normalization in
$\mathbb Z[\beta]$.

Our first main result is uniform over every fixed strictly increasing Pisot
linear numeration system.  If $U=(u_j)$ has $u_0=1$, its canonical greedy
digit set, and its canonical normal words, then there is an effectively
computable $C_U<\infty$ such that
\[
\Phi_{U,m}\text{ injective}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\ell_{\rm cau}(U,m)\le C_Um.
\]
No zero-preservation or Condition~F hypothesis is required.  The proof bounds
all window carries in an explicit finite interval and uses the contracting
Pisot conjugates to collapse adjacent carries; it does not count the
exponentially many difference states.  To the authors' knowledge, this is the
first aperture-linear inverse-depth bound for cyclic language-rank recodings
over fixed Pisot numeration systems.

The same contraction has a formulation independent of this code.  For every
fixed Pisot recurrence $U$ and every bounded integer coefficient alphabet
$[-D,D]$, the finite overlap graph of bounded $U$-representations of zero has
an effective dichotomy: either a first-coordinate-nonzero state reaches a
cycle, or every path from such a state has length $O_{U,D}(m)$.  The order is
sharp for a fixed cubic recurrence with coefficients in $\{-1,0,1\}$.  This
statement uses neither a legal representation language nor a factor code.

The order is sharp in one fixed binary cubic system.  If $\theta$ is the
leading root of $x^3-2x^2+x-1$, then every $m\ge4$ is injective and
\[
\ell_{\rm cau}(\theta,m)=2\lfloor m/2\rfloor-1.
\]
The penultimate collision set is exactly $\{\pm E_m\}$ and the next set is
empty.  Hence no theorem valid for every fixed system in the preceding class
can replace $O_U(m)$ by $o(m)$; this does not claim numerical optimality of
$C_U$ or a universal coefficient.

We also classify all quadratic Pisot bases.  The least nontrivial aperture at
which the sliding recoding is a conjugacy onto its finite-type image is three
exactly for the roots of $x^2-ax-a$ and $x^2-ax+1$, and is two otherwise.
At and above threshold, finite-block injectivity begins at block length $m$,
the natural overlap graph is the right Fischer cover, and the image is
exactly $m$-step, not $(m-1)$-step.  A coordinatewise-difference quotient of
the classical pair graph computes the exact future-only inverse length for every
nonintegral simple-Parry base.  Applied to Bassino's cubic family, it gives
injective apertures with exact inverse lengths tending to infinity.

All claims concern this cyclic language-rank recoding.  They are not claims
about numerical $\beta$-normalization in general, arbitrary
$\beta$-transformations, or all Pisot symbolic dynamics.
\end{abstract}
